Suite score · with plugin79.1%mean of per-case scores
Ablation Δ0.0score points vs baseline, 4 of 4 cases
Baseline score79.1%without the plugin
Cases40 of 4 ≥ 100% threshold
Perfect runs0%runs where every grader passed
How to read this report
  • A run's score is the weighted fraction of its graders that passed; a "perfect" run passed every grader.
  • A case's score is the mean of its runs; a case passes when its score is at or above the 100% threshold (the tick on each case bar).
  • The suite score is the mean of the per-case scores.
  • Judge votes are independent samples of the LLM judge; the majority decides pass or fail.
  • "With plugin" and "Baseline" runs are identical except for the plugin being loaded; Δ is the with-plugin score minus the baseline score.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
## What I assumed you need
- **R1:** specific crop-type labels (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on) per field or pixel.
- **R2:** paired multi-temporal Sentinel-2 data.
- **R3:** a licence that lets you publish results and share trained models.
- **R4:** ready to use, with no download or preprocessing pipeline of your own.
- **Assumption:** you want open downloads you can start on today. All seven below are direct downloads with no account, and each took only a registry-record check.

## Shortlist
Registry records were checked on 2026-11-11.

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | TimeSen2Crop | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 28 GB | Strong |
| 2 | EuroCropsML | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 4.8 GB | Strong |
| 3 | BreizhCrops | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 7.7 GB | Strong |
| 4 | AgriSen-COG | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 28 GB | Strong, with a caveat |
| 5 | PASTIS | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 29 GB | Strong, for segmentation |
| 6 | S2-Agri (Pixel-Set) | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-4.0 | 11 GB | Partial |
| 7 | CropHarvest | Direct download, minutes | CC-BY-SA-4.0 | 28 GB | Partial |

## Details
1. **TimeSen2Crop.** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18681321
   - It has about 1.1 million pixel-level Sentinel-2 series with 16 crop types, one agronomic year, atmospherically corrected, with cloud and snow flags.
   - It covers one region (Austria).
   - It's the simplest fit for per-pixel classification. A Hugging Face copy exists (`monster-monash/TimeSen2Crop`).
   - Paper: Weikmann et al. 2021, doi:10.1109/jstars.2021.3073965.
2. **EuroCropsML.** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.15095445
   - It has 706,683 parcel-level samples across 176 crop classes, as median Sentinel-2 series for Latvia, Portugal and Estonia in 2021.
   - It was built for few-shot, transnational classification.
   - The CC-BY-SA licence means you should release derived data under the same terms. Ask your supervisor whether that also applies to shared model weights.
   - Papers: doi:10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7 (dataset), doi:10.1016/j.ophoto.2026.100117 (baselines).
3. **BreizhCrops.** Download: https://zenodo.org/records/14585341
   - It has field-level series for Brittany, in both top-of-atmosphere and bottom-of-atmosphere versions.
   - The authors provide code and pretrained baselines at github.com/dl4sits/breizhcrops. I did not open the GitHub page.
   - It's well cited (117 citations in the tool's data), which gives you comparison points.
   - Paper: doi:10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020.
4. **AgriSen-COG.** Download: https://zenodo.org/records/7892012
   - It covers five countries (Austria, Belgium, Spain, Denmark and the Netherlands), with labels from farmers' declarations (LPIS).
   - **Caveat:** this Zenodo record holds the labels and rasterised tiles. I did not confirm where the Sentinel-2 imagery is hosted, so check the paper before committing.
   - Paper: doi:10.3390/rs15122980.
5. **PASTIS.** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.5012941
   - It has parcel-level crop segmentation of Sentinel-2 time series in France, with a companion GitHub repository.
   - It suits semantic or panoptic segmentation more than per-pixel-set classification.
6. **S2-Agri (Pixel-Set).** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.5815488
   - It is described as "parcel-based crop type classification", in pixel-set format. A patch version is at doi:10.5281/zenodo.5815523.
   - I did not read its class list, so check that before using it.
7. **CropHarvest.** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10251170
   - It's global, but mostly crop versus non-crop. Only a subset has multi-class crop labels, and it's not Sentinel-2 only. I did not inspect it closely.

On licences, CC-BY-4.0 allows publishing results and sharing models with attribution. I read these from registry metadata, not from the underlying label sources (national LPIS data), so cite both.

## Not recommended, and why
- **ZueriCrop (Hugging Face copy):** it's CC-BY-NC-4.0, and I didn't check the original's terms. The non-commercial clause can complicate sharing models.
- **Germany national crop-type maps (Zenodo 5153047):** these are output maps, not labelled Sentinel-2 time series, and no licence is stated.

## What I could not find
- I verified only a Hugging Face repackaging of Sen4AgriNet (CC-BY-SA-4.0), not the original download.
- I did not find a primary record for DENETHOR.
- The Zenodo API returned invalid responses at times, so coverage may be incomplete.
- I didn't confirm per-dataset class lists, so check that wheat, maize and rapeseed are all present in your region of choice.

## Next step
Start with TimeSen2Crop or BreizhCrops. Both are single-region and well documented, and you can have either within minutes. If you need several countries, use EuroCropsML or AgriSen-COG. Check dataset licences again before publishing, since terms can change.

Interactive page: `dataset-shortlist.html` (in `/private/tmp/e-xF8d4Y/home/cwd`)

7. **CropHarvest.** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10251170
   - It's global, but mostly crop versus non-crop. Only a subset has multi-class crop labels, and it's not Sentinel-2 only. I did not inspect it closely.

On licences, CC-BY-4.0 allows publishing results and sharing models with attribution. I read these from registry metadata, not from the underlying label sources (national LPIS data), so cite both.

## Not recommended, and why
- **ZueriCrop (Hugging Face copy):** it's CC-BY-NC-4.0, and I didn't check the original's terms. The non-commercial clause can complicate sharing models.
- **Germany national crop-type maps (Zenodo 5153047):** these are output maps, not labelled Sentinel-2 time series, and no licence is stated.

## What I could not find
- I verified only a Hugging Face repackaging of Sen4AgriNet (CC-BY-SA-4.0), not the original download.
- I did not find a primary record for DENETHOR.
- The Zenodo API returned invalid responses at times, so coverage may be incomplete.
- I didn't confirm per-dataset class lists, so check that wheat, maize and rapeseed are all present in your region of choice.

## Next step
Start with TimeSen2Crop or BreizhCrops. Both are single-region and well documented, and you can have either within minutes. If you need several countries, use EuroCropsML or AgriSen-COG. Check dataset licences again before publishing, since terms can change.

## Details
1. **TimeSen2Crop.** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18681321
   - It has about 1.1 million pixel-level Sentinel-2 series with 16 crop types, one agronomic year, atmospherically corrected, with cloud and snow flags.
   - It covers one region (Austria).
   - It's the simplest fit for per-pixel classification. A Hugging Face copy exists (`monster-monash/TimeSen2Crop`).
   - Paper: Weikmann et al. 2021, doi:10.1109/jstars.2021.3073965.
2. **EuroCropsML.** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.15095445
   - It has 706,683 parcel-level samples across 176 crop classes, as median Sentinel-2 series for Latvia, Portugal and Estonia in 2021.
   - It was built for few-shot, transnational classification.
   - The CC-BY-SA licence means you should release derived data under the same terms. Ask your supervisor whether that also applies to shared model weights.
   - Papers: doi:10.1038/s41597-025-04952-7 (dataset), doi:10.1016/j.ophoto.2026.100117 (baselines).
3. **BreizhCrops.** Download: https://zenodo.org/records/14585341

   - The authors provide code and pretrained baselines at github.com/dl4sits/breizhcrops. I did not open the GitHub page.
   - It's well cited (117 citations in the tool's data), which gives you comparison points.
   - Paper: doi:10.5194/isprs-archives-xliii-b2-2020-1545-2020.
4. **AgriSen-COG.** Download: https://zenodo.org/records/7892012
   - It covers five countries (Austria, Belgium, Spain, Denmark and the Netherlands), with labels from farmers' declarations (LPIS).
   - **Caveat:** this Zenodo record holds the labels and rasterised tiles. I did not confirm where the Sentinel-2 imagery is hosted, so check the paper before committing.
   - Paper: doi:10.3390/rs15122980.
5. **PASTIS.** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.5012941
   - It has parcel-level crop segmentation of Sentinel-2 time series in France, with a companion GitHub repository.
   - It suits semantic or panoptic segmentation more than per-pixel-set classification.
6. **S2-Agri (Pixel-Set).** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.5815488
   - It is described as "parcel-based crop type classification", in pixel-set format. A patch version is at doi:10.5281/zenodo.5815523.
   - I did not read its class list, so check that before using it.
7. **CropHarvest.** Download: https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.10251170

I'm working from memory and haven't checked the live pages. Confirm each licence on the dataset's own page before you commit to it, because some hosts have moved (Radiant MLHub, for example, migrated to Source Cooperative).

## Best fits

| Dataset | Coverage / labels | S2 pairing | Licence (as I recall) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 2,433 patches of 128×128 px, with parcel-level semantic and panoptic labels for 18 crop classes plus background | Full-year S2 time series per patch (PASTIS-R adds S1) | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo (search "PASTIS"), github.com/VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, about 600k parcels with parcel-level labels (a few dozen classes in the raw data, with a smaller harmonised subset) | Per-parcel S2 time series (L1C and L2A) ready to load | Code is MIT. Labels derive from the French RPG, under Etalab Open Licence, which is CC-BY-compatible. | github.com/dl4sits/BreizhCrops (it has a pip package and a data downloader) |
| **EuroCropsML** | Estonia, Latvia and Portugal, with harmonised EuroCrops labels | Parcel-level S2 time series | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo (search "EuroCropsML") |
| **CropHarvest** | Global point labels, with crop-type labels for a subset | 12-month S2 time series, plus S1 and ERA5 | CC-BY-SA 4.0 on much of it | github.com/nasaharvest/cropharvest, Zenodo |

## Others worth checking

- **Sen4AgriNet** covers Catalonia and France, with parcel-level labels from LPIS data and S2 time series (github.com/Orion-AI-Lab/S4A). I believe it is CC-BY.
- **ZueriCrop** covers Switzerland, with about 48 crop classes and S2 time series, on the ETH Research Collection.
- **TimeMatch** covers several European regions, so it is useful for domain-shift and transfer experiments (Zenodo).
- **Munich480** covers Munich, with 27 classes, but the licence is unclear to me.
- **AgriFieldNet** (India), **South Africa Crop Type** and **DENETHOR** (Germany) were on Radiant MLHub, and I believe all three are CC-BY 4.0.

## Recommendations

- **Pixel- or parcel-sequence classification** (the standard time-series setup): use **BreizhCrops** first. It's the lightest to set up and widely benchmarked.
- **Spatial or semantic segmentation of fields**: use **PASTIS-R**. It has established baselines (U-TAE, for example), so you can compare against published numbers.
- **Generalisation across regions or years**: add **EuroCropsML** or **TimeMatch**. Use **CropHarvest** if you want a global, multi-region test.

## Licence notes

- CC-BY 4.0 lets you publish results and share trained models, as long as you give attribution. That covers PASTIS and EuroCropsML.
- CC-BY-SA (CropHarvest) requires share-alike on derivatives of the data. Whether that applies to model weights is legally unsettled, so if you want a simple position, use CC-BY datasets for the main experiments.
- Sentinel-2 imagery is free and open under Copernicus terms. The label source's licence is what matters in practice, and it is usually the national LPIS/RPG one.
- Some datasets cite or build on others. Check the licence of each source if you combine them.

I can look up the current licence text and download links for any of these if you tell me which ones you're leaning towards.

I'm working from memory and haven't checked the live pages. Confirm each licence on the dataset's own page before you commit to it, because some hosts have moved (Radiant MLHub, for example, migrated to Source Cooperative).

## Best fits

| Dataset | Coverage / labels | S2 pairing | Licence (as I recall) | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PASTIS / PASTIS-R** | France, 2,433 patches of 128×128 px, with parcel-level semantic and panoptic labels for 18 crop classes plus background | Full-year S2 time series per patch (PASTIS-R adds S1) | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo (search "PASTIS"), github.com/VSainteuf/pastis-benchmark |
| **BreizhCrops** | Brittany, France, about 600k parcels with parcel-level labels (a few dozen classes in the raw data, with a smaller harmonised subset) | Per-parcel S2 time series (L1C and L2A) ready to load | Code is MIT. Labels derive from the French RPG, under Etalab Open Licence, which is CC-BY-compatible. | github.com/dl4sits/BreizhCrops (it has a pip package and a data downloader) |
| **EuroCropsML** | Estonia, Latvia and Portugal, with harmonised EuroCrops labels | Parcel-level S2 time series | CC-BY 4.0 | Zenodo (search "EuroCropsML") |
| **CropHarvest** | Global point labels, with crop-type labels for a subset | 12-month S2 time series, plus S1 and ERA5 | CC-BY-SA 4.0 on much of it | github.com/nasaharvest/cropharvest, Zenodo |

## Others worth checking

- **Sen4AgriNet** covers Catalonia and France, with parcel-level labels from LPIS data and S2 time series (github.com/Orion-AI-Lab/S4A). I believe it is CC-BY.
- **ZueriCrop** covers Switzerland, with about 48 crop classes and S2 time series, on the ETH Research Collection.
- **TimeMatch** covers several European regions, so it is useful for domain-shift and transfer experiments (Zenodo).
- **Munich480** covers Munich, with 27 classes, but the licence is unclear to me.
- **AgriFieldNet** (India), **South Africa Crop Type** and **DENETHOR** (Germany) were on Radiant MLHub, and I believe all three are CC-BY 4.0.

## Recommendations

- **Pixel- or parcel-sequence classification** (the standard time-series setup): use **BreizhCrops** first. It's the lightest to set up and widely benchmarked.
- **Spatial or semantic segmentation of fields**: use **PASTIS-R**. It has established baselines (U-TAE, for example), so you can compare against published numbers.
- **Generalisation across regions or years**: add **EuroCropsML** or **TimeMatch**. Use **CropHarvest** if you want a global, multi-region test.

## Licence notes

- CC-BY 4.0 lets you publish results and share trained models, as long as you give attribution. That covers PASTIS and EuroCropsML.
- CC-BY-SA (CropHarvest) requires share-alike on derivatives of the data. Whether that applies to model weights is legally unsettled, so if you want a simple position, use CC-BY datasets for the main experiments.
- Sentinel-2 imagery is free and open under Copernicus terms. The label source's licence is what matters in practice, and it is usually the national LPIS/RPG one.
- Some datasets cite or build on others. Check the licence of each source if you combine them.

I can look up the current licence text and download links for any of these if you tell me which ones you're leaning towards.

Prompt

I'm doing a master's project on crop type classification from Sentinel-2 image time series. I need ground-truth labels for actual crops (wheat, maize, rapeseed and so on, not just cropland versus non-cropland) for individual fields or pixels, already paired with multi-temporal Sentinel-2 data, because I don't have time to build my own download and preprocessing pipeline. The data also needs a licence that lets me publish results and share trained models. Which benchmark datasets fit, and where do I get them?

Graders — what "good" means for this case
00-skill-firedtool_used
tool"Skill"
input_match"\"skill\"\\s*:\\s*\"(?:[\\w-]+:)?dataset-scout\""
01-recall-breizhcropsllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the BreizhCrops dataset (also written Breizhcrops: Sentinel-2 time series of agricultural parcels in Brittany, France, with crop type labels, by Russwurm and colleagues) as a candidate crop type classification benchmark. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-pastisllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the PASTIS dataset (Panoptic Agricultural Satellite TIme Series, by Sainte Fare Garnot and Landrieu; any variant such as PASTIS-R) as a candidate crop type benchmark built on Sentinel-2 time series. FAIL if the answer does not mention PASTIS, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-sen4agrinetllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Sen4AgriNet dataset (also known as S4A, the Sentinel-2 multi-year, multi-country crop classification benchmark covering Catalonia and France, by Sykas and colleagues) as a candidate crop type benchmark. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-recall-timesen2cropllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the TimeSen2Crop dataset (about one million labelled Sentinel-2 time-series samples for 16 crop types in Austria, by Weikmann, Paris and Bruzzone) as a candidate crop type benchmark. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

05-access-pastis-zenodo-cc-byllm

PASS if the final answer states that PASTIS (Panoptic Agricultural Satellite TIme Series) can be downloaded openly, without an application, and that its licence is Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY, version 4.0). Saying that it is hosted on Zenodo is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention PASTIS, gives no licence for it, gives a different licence (for example a non-commercial or share-alike one), or says access must be requested.

06-access-sen4agrinet-mitllm

PASS if the final answer states that Sen4AgriNet (S4A) can be downloaded openly, without an application (for example from the Hugging Face Hub, Dropbox or Google Drive), and that it is released under the MIT licence. FAIL if the answer does not mention Sen4AgriNet, gives no licence for it, gives a different licence, or says access must be requested.

07-access-timesen2crop-zenodo-cc-byllm

PASS if the final answer states that TimeSen2Crop can be downloaded openly, without an application, and that its licence is Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY, version 4.0). Saying that it is hosted on Zenodo is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention TimeSen2Crop, gives no licence for it, gives a different licence, or says access must be requested.

08-trap-eurosat-no-crop-typesllm

PASS if the final answer does not present EuroSAT (the Sentinel-2 land use and land cover patch dataset of 27,000 images in 10 classes, by Helber and colleagues) as providing crop type labels or multi-temporal Sentinel-2 time series. Not mentioning EuroSAT passes. Mentioning it while saying that it has only single-date patches and only broad classes (annual crop, permanent crop, pasture) rather than crop species passes. FAIL if the answer recommends or lists EuroSAT as a crop type classification dataset for this project without stating that limitation.

09-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known crop type datasets that pair labels with Sentinel-2 time series: EuroCropsML (the ready-to-use machine learning version of EuroCrops for Latvia, Portugal and Estonia, by Reuss and colleagues); or the South Africa Crop Type Competition dataset (also called the Western Cape crop type dataset or the Radiant Earth "Spot the Crop" challenge data, on Radiant MLHub or Source Cooperative). FAIL if the answer mentions neither of them.

## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** per-household meter data, hundreds of homes or more.
- **R2 (must):** at least a year of history per household.
- **R3 (must):** hourly or finer.
- **R4:** data you can get now, or via an application, with the licence stated.

I assumed no commercial-use constraint. Checked 2026-11-11. Hugging Face and part of the Zenodo search failed, so coverage is incomplete.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | London Smart Meters (UK Power Networks) | Open download, no sign-in, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 5,567 homes, half-hourly, 2011–14 | Strong |
| 2 | GoiEner Smart Meter Dataset v7.1 (Spain) | Open download on Zenodo, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 28,900 hourly profiles, ~9 GB | Strong, with a caveat |
| 3 | CEEW Smart Meter Data (India) | Open per registry, minutes | CC0 | ~1,000 homes, 3-minute, 2019–21 | Partial |
| 4 | Ausgrid Solar Home (net demand) | Open per registry | CC BY 4.0 | Not confirmed | Partial |
| 5 | CER Irish trial (ISSDA) | **Not confirmed** | **Not confirmed** | Not confirmed | Unknown |
| 6 | Smart-Grid Smart-City (Australia) | **Not confirmed** | **Not confirmed** | Not confirmed | Unknown |
| 7 | Pecan Street Dataport | **Not confirmed** | **Not confirmed** | Not confirmed | Unknown |

## Details

**1. London Smart Meters.** The page is on the [London Datastore](https://data.london.gov.uk/dataset/smartmeter-energy-consumption-data-in-london-households-vqm0d).
- The data is half-hourly for 5,567 households, Nov 2011 to Feb 2014.
- The download is a ~760 MB ZIP holding one ~10 GB CSV of 167M rows. A split version of 168 CSVs is also offered.
- Households joined the trial at different times, so many have under 12 months. Filter by history length. I did not check how many have a full year.
- You need to add weather and holidays yourself.
- Used for day-ahead forecasting in [Benchmarking of Load Forecasting Methods Using Residential Smart Meter Data](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12199844) (2022), on 71 selected households.

**2. GoiEner v7.1.** The record is on [Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22313409).
- It holds hourly profiles from a Spanish energy cooperative, with consumption, generation and import/export variables.
- The page doesn't state the years covered or how many customers have a full year. The annual matrix has a `full_year` flag you can filter on.
- The metadata carries a business-activity code (CNAE), so some customers may be businesses, not homes. Check this before benchmarking.

**3. CEEW India.** The record is [here](https://doi.org/10.71646/gexzrr).
- It has 3-minute readings from ~1,000 households in Mathura and Bareilly.
- The registry says open and CC0, but I couldn't open the landing page or the Dataverse copy, so confirm in a browser.
- The usage patterns are unlike European homes. Per-household history length is unchecked.

**4. Ausgrid Solar Home.** The record is [here](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32975426.v1).
- The registry says open and CC BY 4.0, but figshare blocked my page read, so I haven't seen the contents.
- From memory it is ~300 homes at half-hourly resolution, which I did not verify.
- It is net demand with rooftop solar, so the target mixes load and generation.

**5–7. CER, SGSC and Pecan Street.** All three exist as records, but I could not read their access pages.
- **CER:** the DOI is registered ([record](https://doi.org/10.7929/issda/bx59eu)). The ISSDA site returned errors. I recall an application form and agreement, and about 5,000 homes and businesses at half-hourly resolution over ~18 months. That is unconfirmed, so write to issda@ucd.ie.
- **SGSC:** the data.gov.au page exists, but I got only its metadata. Access and licence are unread.
- **Pecan Street:** the registry gives no access or licence. I recall academic access on application and paid commercial access, but I didn't verify that.

## Not recommended, and why

- **SERL (UK):** the open pages are aggregated statistics only. The household-level data appears to be safeguarded access via the UK Data Service. Its page was blocked, so this is unverified.
- **Synthetic profiles, appliance-level sets and feeder or commercial datasets** turned up in the search. They are synthetic, too small, or not household-level.

## What I could not find

- Confirmed access terms for CER, SGSC and Pecan Street.
- Confirmed counts of households with 12+ months of data in any dataset.

## Next step

Start with London and GoiEner, which are open today and large. Then count the households with 12+ months of history and drop non-household customers from GoiEner. Confirm CER's access terms with ISSDA if you want its trial data.

Interactive page: `/private/tmp/e-SXQ4TA/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`

- **SERL (UK):** the open pages are aggregated statistics only. The household-level data appears to be safeguarded access via the UK Data Service. Its page was blocked, so this is unverified.
- **Synthetic profiles, appliance-level sets and feeder or commercial datasets** turned up in the search. They are synthetic, too small, or not household-level.

## Details

**1. London Smart Meters.** The page is on the [London Datastore](https://data.london.gov.uk/dataset/smartmeter-energy-consumption-data-in-london-households-vqm0d).
- The data is half-hourly for 5,567 households, Nov 2011 to Feb 2014.
- The download is a ~760 MB ZIP holding one ~10 GB CSV of 167M rows. A split version of 168 CSVs is also offered.
- Households joined the trial at different times, so many have under 12 months. Filter by history length. I did not check how many have a full year.
- You need to add weather and holidays yourself.
- Used for day-ahead forecasting in [Benchmarking of Load Forecasting Methods Using Residential Smart Meter Data](https://doi.org/10.3390/app12199844) (2022), on 71 selected households.

**2. GoiEner v7.1.** The record is on [Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22313409).
- It holds hourly profiles from a Spanish energy cooperative, with consumption, generation and import/export variables.
- The page doesn't state the years covered or how many customers have a full year. The annual matrix has a `full_year` flag you can filter on.
- The metadata carries a business-activity code (CNAE), so some customers may be businesses, not homes. Check this before benchmarking.

**3. CEEW India.** The record is [here](https://doi.org/10.71646/gexzrr).
- It has 3-minute readings from ~1,000 households in Mathura and Bareilly.
- The registry says open and CC0, but I couldn't open the landing page or the Dataverse copy, so confirm in a browser.
- The usage patterns are unlike European homes. Per-household history length is unchecked.

**4. Ausgrid Solar Home.** The record is [here](https://doi.org/10.6084/m9.figshare.32975426.v1).
- The registry says open and CC BY 4.0, but figshare blocked my page read, so I haven't seen the contents.
- From memory it is ~300 homes at half-hourly resolution, which I did not verify.
- It is net demand with rooftop solar, so the target mixes load and generation.

**5–7. CER, SGSC and Pecan Street.** All three exist as records, but I could not read their access pages.
- **CER:** the DOI is registered ([record](https://doi.org/10.7929/issda/bx59eu)). The ISSDA site returned errors. I recall an application form and agreement, and about 5,000 homes and businesses at half-hourly resolution over ~18 months. That is unconfirmed, so write to issda@ucd.ie.
- **SGSC:** the data.gov.au page exists, but I got only its metadata. Access and licence are unread.
- **Pecan Street:** the registry gives no access or licence. I recall academic access on application and paid commercial access, but I didn't verify that.

## Not recommended, and why

- **SERL (UK):** the open pages are aggregated statistics only. The household-level data appears to be safeguarded access via the UK Data Service. Its page was blocked, so this is unverified.
- **Synthetic profiles, appliance-level sets and feeder or commercial datasets** turned up in the search. They are synthetic, too small, or not household-level.

✓ pass 01-recall-low-carbon-london

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Several fit your criteria, and some need no application. This is from memory and I haven't checked the current pages, so confirm sizes, dates and licenses before you commit.

## Direct download, no application

| Dataset | Households | Resolution | History | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **GoiEner** (Basque Country, Zenodo) | ~25,000 | Hourly | Up to several years, varying per customer | Largest open one. It includes metadata such as tariff and postcode area, and the license is CC BY. Some customers have rooftop PV. |
| **UK Power Networks "Low Carbon London"** (London Datastore, mirrored on Kaggle) | ~5,500 | 30 min | Nov 2011 to Feb 2014 | Households have different start and end dates. About 1,100 were on a dynamic time-of-use tariff, so filter or flag them. |
| **Ausgrid Solar Home** (Sydney) | 300 | 30 min | 3 years | Every home has rooftop PV. Consumption and generation are separate columns, which is useful but unrepresentative of homes without PV. |
| **IDEAL** (Edinburgh DataShare) | 255 | Fine, down to seconds in the raw files | ~2 years | Smaller, with rich metadata and surveys. |

## Registration or application needed

- **Irish CER Smart Metering Trial** (via ISSDA, Irish Social Science Data Archive): ~5,000 homes and SMEs at 30 min, about 1.5 years from 2009 to 2010. You fill in a request form and sign a data-use agreement, and academic use is typically free. It's the classic benchmark, with survey data to separate residential from business meters.
- **Pecan Street Dataport**: hundreds of homes at 1-minute to 15-minute resolution, with circuit-level data. Academic access is free but needs an application, and commercial use is paid. Coverage and history length vary a lot by home.
- **UK Data Service** (for example, the Energy Demand Research Project): needs registration and has special-license tiers.

## Probably not what you need
- **UCI ElectricityLoadDiagrams (370 clients)**: widely used, but it's mostly businesses and a mix of customers, not clearly homes.
- **REFIT, UK-DALE, Smart\* and similar**: too few homes, typically 5–115.
- **NREL ResStock/EULP**: simulated, not measured.

## Recommendation
Start with **GoiEner** and **London**. Both are large and need no gatekeeping, and they cover different countries, climates and tariff regimes. Add **CER** if you want the standard benchmark, since requests are usually approved within days to weeks.

Some things will affect your benchmark design:
- **Weather and calendar covariates aren't included.** You'll need to join them yourself, for example from Open-Meteo or ERA5 for GoiEner and London.
- **Missing data and varying household histories** mean you should decide how to filter, for example by requiring at least a year of near-complete data.
- **Rooftop PV** in GoiEner and Ausgrid makes net load very different from gross demand, so treat those households as a separate group.
- **Dates are old** for CER and London, so some behavior, like EV charging and heat pumps, is absent.

I can fetch the current landing pages to verify license and access details, or write a loader and filtering script for one of these.

## Direct download, no application

| Dataset | Households | Resolution | History | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **GoiEner** (Basque Country, Zenodo) | ~25,000 | Hourly | Up to several years, varying per customer | Largest open one. It includes metadata such as tariff and postcode area, and the license is CC BY. Some customers have rooftop PV. |
| **UK Power Networks "Low Carbon London"** (London Datastore, mirrored on Kaggle) | ~5,500 | 30 min | Nov 2011 to Feb 2014 | Households have different start and end dates. About 1,100 were on a dynamic time-of-use tariff, so filter or flag them. |

## Registration or application needed

- **Irish CER Smart Metering Trial** (via ISSDA, Irish Social Science Data Archive): ~5,000 homes and SMEs at 30 min, about 1.5 years from 2009 to 2010. You fill in a request form and sign a data-use agreement, and academic use is typically free. It's the classic benchmark, with survey data to separate residential from business meters.
- **Pecan Street Dataport**: hundreds of homes at 1-minute to 15-minute resolution, with circuit-level data. Academic access is free but needs an application, and commercial use is paid. Coverage and history length vary a lot by home.
- **UK Data Service** (for example, the Energy Demand Research Project): needs registration and has special-license tiers.

Prompt

I want to benchmark day-ahead electricity demand forecasting for individual homes, so I need smart-meter consumption data per household, not feeder or grid totals. Ideally hundreds of households or more, at least a year of history each, at hourly resolution or finer. What public datasets are there, and can I just download them or do I have to apply?

Graders — what "good" means for this case
00-skill-firedtool_used
tool"Skill"
input_match"\"skill\"\\s*:\\s*\"(?:[\\w-]+:)?dataset-scout\""
01-recall-low-carbon-londonllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the London smart meter dataset from the Low Carbon London project (also known as "SmartMeter Energy Consumption Data in London Households", LCL, the UK Power Networks London smart meter data on the London Datastore, or the Kaggle "Smart meters in London" copy) as a candidate household electricity dataset. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-cer-irish-smart-meterllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Irish CER Smart Metering Project electricity data (also known as the CER Electricity Customer Behaviour Trial 2009-2010, the Irish Commission for Energy Regulation smart meter data, or the ISSDA smart meter data) as a candidate household electricity dataset. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-smart-grid-smart-cityllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Smart-Grid Smart-City Customer Trial Data (also known as SGSC, the Ausgrid-led Smart Grid Smart City trial from New South Wales, Australia, published on data.gov.au) as a candidate household electricity dataset. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-recall-pecan-streetllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists Pecan Street Dataport (also known as Pecan Street, the Pecan Street Inc. residential energy data from Austin, Texas and other testbeds) as a candidate household electricity dataset. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

05-access-low-carbon-london-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that the Low Carbon London smart meter data ("SmartMeter Energy Consumption Data in London Households", LCL) can be downloaded directly, without an application, from the London Datastore. If the answer names its licence, the licence must be Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY, version 4.0 on the London Datastore page). FAIL if the answer does not mention this dataset, says it must be applied for, or gives a different licence (for example a non-commercial one).

06-access-cer-issda-requestllm

PASS if the final answer states that the Irish CER Smart Metering Project electricity data is not an open download but must be requested from the Irish Social Science Data Archive (ISSDA), for example by submitting an ISSDA data request form that is reviewed before access is granted, and that it is provided for non-commercial research or teaching. Mentioning the ISSDA end user licence, or that ISSDA supplies data only for use in the EEA and countries with an EU adequacy decision, is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention the CER data, describes it as freely downloadable, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

07-access-pecan-street-free-tier-limitedllm

PASS if the final answer states that Pecan Street Dataport requires an account and a licence, and that free access for university researchers covers only a limited subset of homes (75 homes: 25 each from Austin, New York and California) while the full datasets require a paid licence. An answer that conveys either the limited free subset or the paid licence for the full data passes. FAIL if the answer does not mention Pecan Street, describes the full dataset as freely downloadable or free for all academic use without any limitation, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

08-access-smart-grid-smart-city-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that the Smart-Grid Smart-City (SGSC) Customer Trial Data can be downloaded directly, without an application, from data.gov.au. If the answer names its licence, the licence must be Creative Commons Attribution (CC BY 3.0 Australia). FAIL if the answer does not mention this dataset, says it must be applied for, or gives a different licence.

09-trap-uci-individual-householdllm

PASS if the final answer does not present the UCI "Individual household electric power consumption" dataset (one-minute measurements from a single house in Sceaux near Paris, December 2006 to November 2010) as meeting the requirement of many households. Not mentioning it passes. Mentioning it while saying that it covers only one household passes. FAIL if the answer recommends or lists this dataset as a multi-household dataset, or lists it as a candidate for this many-household benchmark without saying that it contains a single household.

10-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known household electricity datasets: the IDEAL household energy dataset (255 homes in and around Edinburgh, Scotland, on Edinburgh DataShare); or the GoiEner smart meter data (hourly consumption from customers of the Spanish electricity cooperative GoiEner, on Zenodo). FAIL if the answer mentions neither of them.

✓ pass 01-recall-physionet-2019-sepsis-challenge

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** A time-stamped sepsis onset, either given (Sepsis-3 or close) or derivable.
- **R2 (must):** Routinely charted vitals and labs over time.
- **R3 (must):** Adult ICU patients, thousands or more.
- **R4 (nice to have):** A first baseline possible within a week.

I assumed you have a supervisor or colleague who can vouch for you and a university email address. I also assumed you can't run restricted data through hosted AI services (see the PhysioNet note under MIMIC-IV).

I checked the registries on 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12. Access rules change, so check each page again before you apply.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019 | **Open download, minutes** | CC-BY-4.0 | 40,336 ICU patients (two public hospital systems), hourly | Strong. Onset label is given. |
| 2 | MIMIC-IV 3.1 | Credentialed, days to weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed DUA | 94,458 ICU stays, 65,366 patients (Boston, 2008–2022) | Strong. You derive the label. |
| 3 | eICU-CRD | Credentialed (same application as #2) | PhysioNet Credentialed DUA | 200k+ unit stays, 208 US hospitals, 2014–2015 | Strong for external validation. You derive the label. |
| 4 | HiRID 1.1.1 | Credentialed plus per-study review by the authors, weeks | PhysioNet Contributor Review DUA | ~34k admissions (Bern, 2008–2016), 681 variables | Strong. You derive the label. |
| 5 | AmsterdamUMCdb | Application at amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl; steps not confirmed | DANS licence | 23,106 admissions (2003–2016) | Partial. You derive the label. |
| 6 | SICdb 1.0.8 | Credentialed plus per-study review by the authors, weeks | PhysioNet Contributor Review DUA | 27k+ admissions (Salzburg, 2013–2021) | Partial. You derive the label. |
| 7 | MIMIC-IV Demo | Open | ODbL-1.0 | Demo subset | Pipeline development only |

## Details

**Start today: Challenge 2019** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/challenge-2019/1.0.0/))
- **Contents:** 40 variables per hour: 8 vitals, 26 labs and 6 demographic or unit fields, plus a `SepsisLabel`. The label follows Sepsis-3: a 2-point SOFA change plus suspected infection (cultures or IV antibiotics). Onset is the earlier of the two times.
- **Third hospital:** A third hospital system is hidden and used for scoring.
- **Caveat on the label:** The page says the training labels are shifted 6 hours earlier. Check how that fits your target definition.
- **Limits:** There are no raw medications or cultures, so you can't rederive onset with a different Sepsis-3 variant. It is also heavily used, so novelty has to come from your method or evaluation.
- **Access:** None needed. Download it.

**Apply now: MIMIC-IV** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciv/3.1/))
- **Contents:** Charted vitals, labs, inputs, cultures and prescriptions, so you can derive Sepsis-3 onset yourself. The page has no sepsis label.
- **Access steps:**
  1. Create a PhysioNet account.
  2. Complete CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" training.
  3. Submit the credentialing application, which includes references.
  4. Sign the Credentialed DUA on the dataset page.
- **Time:** I couldn't confirm a typical approval time from the pages. The CITI course takes a few hours.
- **Personal access:** Access is per person. Your teammates apply separately.
- **AI services:** The agreement bars sending the data through APIs or online platforms. PhysioNet recommends locally run models ([their post](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/)). Don't paste records into hosted tools.
- **Related paper:** MIMIC-Sepsis ([doi](https://doi.org/10.1109/bhi67747.2025.11269536)) is a curated cohort of 35,239 ICU patients from MIMIC-IV. I only read its abstract and didn't confirm where its code is released.

**eICU-CRD** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/eicu-crd/2.0/))
- It uses the same credentialing as MIMIC-IV, so one application covers both.
- It has no sepsis label, and charting varies by hospital.
- It is mainly useful for external validation.

**HiRID and SICdb**
- Both need credentialing, plus a specific research question submitted to the dataset authors.
- Neither page states a turnaround time.
- Neither has a sepsis label.
- HiRID is ~18 GB.

**AmsterdamUMCdb** ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.17026/dans-22u-f8vd))
- The README sends you to amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl.
- I couldn't load that site, so steps, eligibility, cost and timing are unconfirmed.
- A Sepsis-3 implementation for this dataset exists ([supporting data](https://doi.org/10.5524/102204)).

**Multi-dataset benchmark**
- The YAIB framework supports MIMIC-III/IV, eICU, HiRID and AmsterdamUMCdb, and sepsis is one of its five predefined tasks ([paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.05109), [code](https://github.com/rvandewater/YAIB)).
- I didn't confirm how it defines sepsis. It may save you from writing your own cohort code.
- A related paper pooled four public ICU datasets (334,812 stays) to test how well models transfer to new hospitals ([doi](https://doi.org/10.1097/ccm.0000000000006359)). I read only its abstract and didn't confirm that sepsis is one of its tasks.

## Not recommended, and why

- **WiDS Datathon 2020:** It predicts mortality, not timed sepsis onset.
- **Health Gym synthetic sepsis data:** It is synthetic, derived from MIMIC-III, and credentialed anyway.
- **Hugging Face copies of the Challenge 2019 data:** They are unofficial mirrors. Use the PhysioNet original.
- **Neonatal and omics sepsis sets on Zenodo:** They are the wrong population or modality.

## What I could not find

- I couldn't confirm real-world approval times for PhysioNet credentialing or the HiRID and SICdb author reviews. Ask people in your lab who applied recently.
- I couldn't load the AmsterdamUMCdb application page.
- I didn't confirm where MIMIC-Sepsis releases its code or cohort.
- Some registry queries failed (DataCite, Zenodo, Hugging Face), so coverage may be incomplete.
- I didn't assess non-Western ICU databases, such as the Zhejiang critical care EHR set that showed up in results.

## Next step

1. **Today:** Download Challenge 2019 and build your baseline on it. Start the CITI training and the PhysioNet credentialing application, and ask your supervisor to confirm the reference. Also ask your institution whether this work needs an ethics exemption.
2. **While waiting:** Use the MIMIC-IV demo to write your Sepsis-3 derivation pipeline. Then apply for HiRID once you have a concrete research question.
3. **After approval:** Train on MIMIC-IV and validate externally on eICU or HiRID.

Interactive page: `/private/tmp/e-b61852/home/cwd/dataset-shortlist.html`

- **Contents:** Charted vitals, labs, inputs, cultures and prescriptions, so you can derive Sepsis-3 onset yourself. The page has no sepsis label.
- **Access steps:**
  1. Create a PhysioNet account.
  2. Complete CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" training.
  3. Submit the credentialing application, which includes references.
  4. Sign the Credentialed DUA on the dataset page.
- **Time:** I couldn't confirm a typical approval time from the pages. The CITI course takes a few hours.
- **Personal access:** Access is per person. Your teammates apply separately.
- **AI services:** The agreement bars sending the data through APIs or online platforms. PhysioNet recommends locally run models ([their post](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/)). Don't paste records into hosted tools.
- **Related paper:** MIMIC-Sepsis ([doi](https://doi.org/10.1109/bhi67747.2025.11269536)) is a curated cohort of 35,239 ICU patients from MIMIC-IV. I only read its abstract and didn't confirm where its code is released.

**eICU-CRD** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/eicu-crd/2.0/))
- It uses the same credentialing as MIMIC-IV, so one application covers both.
- It has no sepsis label, and charting varies by hospital.
- It is mainly useful for external validation.

**HiRID and SICdb**
- Both need credentialing, plus a specific research question submitted to the dataset authors.
- Neither page states a turnaround time.
- Neither has a sepsis label.
- HiRID is ~18 GB.

**AmsterdamUMCdb** ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.17026/dans-22u-f8vd))
- The README sends you to amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl.
- I couldn't load that site, so steps, eligibility, cost and timing are unconfirmed.
- A Sepsis-3 implementation for this dataset exists ([supporting data](https://doi.org/10.5524/102204)).

**Multi-dataset benchmark**
- The YAIB framework supports MIMIC-III/IV, eICU, HiRID and AmsterdamUMCdb, and sepsis is one of its five predefined tasks ([paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.05109), [code](https://github.com/rvandewater/YAIB)).
- I didn't confirm how it defines sepsis. It may save you from writing your own cohort code.
- A related paper pooled four public ICU datasets (334,812 stays) to test how well models transfer to new hospitals ([doi](https://doi.org/10.1097/ccm.0000000000006359)). I read only its abstract and didn't confirm that sepsis is one of its tasks.

## Not recommended, and why

- **WiDS Datathon 2020:** It predicts mortality, not timed sepsis onset.
- **Health Gym synthetic sepsis data:** It is synthetic, derived from MIMIC-III, and credentialed anyway.
- **Hugging Face copies of the Challenge 2019 data:** They are unofficial mirrors. Use the PhysioNet original.
- **Neonatal and omics sepsis sets on Zenodo:** They are the wrong population or modality.

## What I could not find

- I couldn't confirm real-world approval times for PhysioNet credentialing or the HiRID and SICdb author reviews. Ask people in your lab who applied recently.
- I couldn't load the AmsterdamUMCdb application page.
- I didn't confirm where MIMIC-Sepsis releases its code or cohort.
- Some registry queries failed (DataCite, Zenodo, Hugging Face), so coverage may be incomplete.
- I didn't assess non-Western ICU databases, such as the Zhejiang critical care EHR set that showed up in results.

## Next step

1. **Today:** Download Challenge 2019 and build your baseline on it. Start the CITI training and the PhysioNet credentialing application, and ask your supervisor to confirm the reference. Also ask your institution whether this work needs an ethics exemption.
2. **While waiting:** Use the MIMIC-IV demo to write your Sepsis-3 derivation pipeline. Then apply for HiRID once you have a concrete research question.
3. **After approval:** Train on MIMIC-IV and validate externally on eICU or HiRID.

## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** A time-stamped sepsis onset, either given (Sepsis-3 or close) or derivable.
- **R2 (must):** Routinely charted vitals and labs over time.
- **R3 (must):** Adult ICU patients, thousands or more.
- **R4 (nice to have):** A first baseline possible within a week.

I assumed you have a supervisor or colleague who can vouch for you and a university email address. I also assumed you can't run restricted data through hosted AI services (see the PhysioNet note under MIMIC-IV).

I checked the registries on 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12. Access rules change, so check each page again before you apply.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019 | **Open download, minutes** | CC-BY-4.0 | 40,336 ICU patients (two public hospital systems), hourly | Strong. Onset label is given. |
| 2 | MIMIC-IV 3.1 | Credentialed, days to weeks | PhysioNet Credentialed DUA | 94,458 ICU stays, 65,366 patients (Boston, 2008–2022) | Strong. You derive the label. |
| 3 | eICU-CRD | Credentialed (same application as #2) | PhysioNet Credentialed DUA | 200k+ unit stays, 208 US hospitals, 2014–2015 | Strong for external validation. You derive the label. |
| 4 | HiRID 1.1.1 | Credentialed plus per-study review by the authors, weeks | PhysioNet Contributor Review DUA | ~34k admissions (Bern, 2008–2016), 681 variables | Strong. You derive the label. |
| 5 | AmsterdamUMCdb | Application at amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl; steps not confirmed | DANS licence | 23,106 admissions (2003–2016) | Partial. You derive the label. |
| 6 | SICdb 1.0.8 | Credentialed plus per-study review by the authors, weeks | PhysioNet Contributor Review DUA | 27k+ admissions (Salzburg, 2013–2021) | Partial. You derive the label. |
| 7 | MIMIC-IV Demo | Open | ODbL-1.0 | Demo subset | Pipeline development only |

## Details

**Start today: Challenge 2019** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/challenge-2019/1.0.0/))
- **Contents:** 40 variables per hour: 8 vitals, 26 labs and 6 demographic or unit fields, plus a `SepsisLabel`. The label follows Sepsis-3: a 2-point SOFA change plus suspected infection (cultures or IV antibiotics). Onset is the earlier of the two times.
- **Third hospital:** A third hospital system is hidden and used for scoring.
- **Caveat on the label:** The page says the training labels are shifted 6 hours earlier. Check how that fits your target definition.
- **Limits:** There are no raw medications or cultures, so you can't rederive onset with a different Sepsis-3 variant. It is also heavily used, so novelty has to come from your method or evaluation.
- **Access:** None needed. Download it.

**Apply now: MIMIC-IV** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciv/3.1/))
- **Contents:** Charted vitals, labs, inputs, cultures and prescriptions, so you can derive Sepsis-3 onset yourself. The page has no sepsis label.
- **Access steps:**
  1. Create a PhysioNet account.
  2. Complete CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" training.
  3. Submit the credentialing application, which includes references.
  4. Sign the Credentialed DUA on the dataset page.
- **Time:** I couldn't confirm a typical approval time from the pages. The CITI course takes a few hours.
- **Personal access:** Access is per person. Your teammates apply separately.
- **AI services:** The agreement bars sending the data through APIs or online platforms. PhysioNet recommends locally run models ([their post](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/)). Don't paste records into hosted tools.
- **Related paper:** MIMIC-Sepsis ([doi](https://doi.org/10.1109/bhi67747.2025.11269536)) is a curated cohort of 35,239 ICU patients from MIMIC-IV. I only read its abstract and didn't confirm where its code is released.

**eICU-CRD** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/eicu-crd/2.0/))
- It uses the same credentialing as MIMIC-IV, so one application covers both.
- It has no sepsis label, and charting varies by hospital.
- It is mainly useful for external validation.

**HiRID and SICdb**
- Both need credentialing, plus a specific research question submitted to the dataset authors.
- Neither page states a turnaround time.
- Neither has a sepsis label.
- HiRID is ~18 GB.

**AmsterdamUMCdb** ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.17026/dans-22u-f8vd))
- The README sends you to amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl.
- I couldn't load that site, so steps, eligibility, cost and timing are unconfirmed.

- **AI services:** The agreement bars sending the data through APIs or online platforms. PhysioNet recommends locally run models ([their post](https://physionet.org/news/post/llm-responsible-use/)). Don't paste records into hosted tools.
- **Related paper:** MIMIC-Sepsis ([doi](https://doi.org/10.1109/bhi67747.2025.11269536)) is a curated cohort of 35,239 ICU patients from MIMIC-IV. I only read its abstract and didn't confirm where its code is released.

**eICU-CRD** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/eicu-crd/2.0/))
- It uses the same credentialing as MIMIC-IV, so one application covers both.
- It has no sepsis label, and charting varies by hospital.
- It is mainly useful for external validation.

**HiRID and SICdb**
- Both need credentialing, plus a specific research question submitted to the dataset authors.
- Neither page states a turnaround time.
- Neither has a sepsis label.
- HiRID is ~18 GB.

**AmsterdamUMCdb** ([DOI](https://doi.org/10.17026/dans-22u-f8vd))
- The README sends you to amsterdammedicaldatascience.nl.
- I couldn't load that site, so steps, eligibility, cost and timing are unconfirmed.
- A Sepsis-3 implementation for this dataset exists ([supporting data](https://doi.org/10.5524/102204)).

**Multi-dataset benchmark**
- The YAIB framework supports MIMIC-III/IV, eICU, HiRID and AmsterdamUMCdb, and sepsis is one of its five predefined tasks ([paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.05109), [code](https://github.com/rvandewater/YAIB)).
- I didn't confirm how it defines sepsis. It may save you from writing your own cohort code.
- A related paper pooled four public ICU datasets (334,812 stays) to test how well models transfer to new hospitals ([doi](https://doi.org/10.1097/ccm.0000000000006359)). I read only its abstract and didn't confirm that sepsis is one of its tasks.

## Not recommended, and why

- **WiDS Datathon 2020:** It predicts mortality, not timed sepsis onset.
- **Health Gym synthetic sepsis data:** It is synthetic, derived from MIMIC-III, and credentialed anyway.
- **Hugging Face copies of the Challenge 2019 data:** They are unofficial mirrors. Use the PhysioNet original.
- **Neonatal and omics sepsis sets on Zenodo:** They are the wrong population or modality.

- I couldn't confirm real-world approval times for PhysioNet credentialing or the HiRID and SICdb author reviews. Ask people in your lab who applied recently.

## Details

**Start today: Challenge 2019** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/challenge-2019/1.0.0/))
- **Contents:** 40 variables per hour: 8 vitals, 26 labs and 6 demographic or unit fields, plus a `SepsisLabel`. The label follows Sepsis-3: a 2-point SOFA change plus suspected infection (cultures or IV antibiotics). Onset is the earlier of the two times.
- **Third hospital:** A third hospital system is hidden and used for scoring.
- **Caveat on the label:** The page says the training labels are shifted 6 hours earlier. Check how that fits your target definition.
- **Limits:** There are no raw medications or cultures, so you can't rederive onset with a different Sepsis-3 variant. It is also heavily used, so novelty has to come from your method or evaluation.
- **Access:** None needed. Download it.

**Apply now: MIMIC-IV** ([link](https://physionet.org/content/mimiciv/3.1/))

  2. Complete CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" training.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Start with the PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019 data on day one. Apply for MIMIC-IV and eICU in parallel, because both need credentialing. This is from memory and I haven't checked the current pages, so confirm turnaround times and versions on each site.

## Download now (no application)

**PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019, "Early Prediction of Sepsis from Clinical Data"**
- It has about 40k ICU stays from two hospital systems (training sets A and B), with hourly vitals and labs and 40 variables.
- It comes with a ready-made time-stamped `SepsisLabel`, derived using Sepsis-3 criteria. That makes it a first baseline you can run this week.
- The label is deliberately shifted: it turns positive 6 hours before the computed onset. Account for that when you compare against other datasets.
- The test set (set C) is hidden, so you'll cross-validate or split across A and B.
- It doesn't include antibiotic or culture timestamps, so you can't re-derive onset under a different definition.
- The eICU and MIMIC demo subsets are also open, but at about 100 patients they're only good for testing code.

## Credentialed (PhysioNet)

These all share one process:
1. Make a PhysioNet account.
2. Complete CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" training and upload the certificate.
3. Submit a credentialing application, which needs a reference (your supervisor, usually).
4. After approval, sign a separate data use agreement for each dataset.

Credentialing review typically takes from several days to a couple of weeks. The CITI course takes a few hours. Start today, because this is the step most likely to eat your week.

| Dataset | Notes for sepsis onset |
|---|---|
| **MIMIC-IV** | The best second dataset. It has antibiotic, culture, and SOFA inputs, and the `mimic-code` repo has a `sepsis3` derived concept (suspected infection plus SOFA ≥ 2) that gives onset times. |
| **eICU-CRD** | Multicenter, about 200 US hospitals. Good for external validation, but derivation is messier because medication and culture timing are less clean. |
| **HiRID** | A high-resolution Swiss ICU dataset. You derive onset yourself. |
| **SICdb** | An Austrian ICU dataset. You derive onset yourself. |

If you'd rather not write the Sepsis-3 derivation yourself, look at the **YAIB (Yet Another ICU Benchmark)** codebase. It has a sepsis task with a harmonized pipeline across MIMIC, eICU, HiRID, and AmsterdamUMCdb, which saves a lot of time.

## Separate application

**AmsterdamUMCdb** is not on the PhysioNet credentialing path. It goes through Amsterdam Medical Data Science, with a data use agreement and institutional sign-off. Expect a few weeks. It's worth doing later as an external-validation set, not for the first week.

## Suggested plan

- **Today:** download Challenge 2019, and start the CITI training and PhysioNet credentialing.
- **Days 1–5:** build the baseline on Challenge 2019, for example gradient-boosted trees on hourly windowed features, evaluated with the Challenge utility score as well as AUROC.
- **Once approved:** add MIMIC-IV with the `sepsis3` concept, then eICU or HiRID for external validation.

PhysioNet's MIMIC and eICU terms restrict sending the data to external LLM APIs, so use opt-out or private deployments if you use AI tools on it. Check each dataset's current policy.

If you'd like, I can look up the current application pages and turnaround times, or sketch the MIMIC-IV onset derivation.

**PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019, "Early Prediction of Sepsis from Clinical Data"**
- It has about 40k ICU stays from two hospital systems (training sets A and B), with hourly vitals and labs and 40 variables.
- It comes with a ready-made time-stamped `SepsisLabel`, derived using Sepsis-3 criteria. That makes it a first baseline you can run this week.
- The label is deliberately shifted: it turns positive 6 hours before the computed onset. Account for that when you compare against other datasets.
- The test set (set C) is hidden, so you'll cross-validate or split across A and B.
- It doesn't include antibiotic or culture timestamps, so you can't re-derive onset under a different definition.
- The eICU and MIMIC demo subsets are also open, but at about 100 patients they're only good for testing code.

## Credentialed (PhysioNet)

These all share one process:
1. Make a PhysioNet account.
2. Complete CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" training and upload the certificate.
3. Submit a credentialing application, which needs a reference (your supervisor, usually).
4. After approval, sign a separate data use agreement for each dataset.

Credentialing review typically takes from several days to a couple of weeks. The CITI course takes a few hours. Start today, because this is the step most likely to eat your week.

| Dataset | Notes for sepsis onset |
|---|---|
| **MIMIC-IV** | The best second dataset. It has antibiotic, culture, and SOFA inputs, and the `mimic-code` repo has a `sepsis3` derived concept (suspected infection plus SOFA ≥ 2) that gives onset times. |
| **eICU-CRD** | Multicenter, about 200 US hospitals. Good for external validation, but derivation is messier because medication and culture timing are less clean. |
| **HiRID** | A high-resolution Swiss ICU dataset. You derive onset yourself. |
| **SICdb** | An Austrian ICU dataset. You derive onset yourself. |

If you'd rather not write the Sepsis-3 derivation yourself, look at the **YAIB (Yet Another ICU Benchmark)** codebase. It has a sepsis task with a harmonized pipeline across MIMIC, eICU, HiRID, and AmsterdamUMCdb, which saves a lot of time.

## Separate application

**AmsterdamUMCdb** is not on the PhysioNet credentialing path. It goes through Amsterdam Medical Data Science, with a data use agreement and institutional sign-off. Expect a few weeks. It's worth doing later as an external-validation set, not for the first week.

## Suggested plan

- **Today:** download Challenge 2019, and start the CITI training and PhysioNet credentialing.
- **Days 1–5:** build the baseline on Challenge 2019, for example gradient-boosted trees on hourly windowed features, evaluated with the Challenge utility score as well as AUROC.
- **Once approved:** add MIMIC-IV with the `sepsis3` concept, then eICU or HiRID for external validation.

PhysioNet's MIMIC and eICU terms restrict sending the data to external LLM APIs, so use opt-out or private deployments if you use AI tools on it. Check each dataset's current policy.

If you'd like, I can look up the current application pages and turnaround times, or sketch the MIMIC-IV onset derivation.

## Separate application

**AmsterdamUMCdb** is not on the PhysioNet credentialing path. It goes through Amsterdam Medical Data Science, with a data use agreement and institutional sign-off. Expect a few weeks. It's worth doing later as an external-validation set, not for the first week.

## Suggested plan

- **Today:** download Challenge 2019, and start the CITI training and PhysioNet credentialing.
- **Days 1–5:** build the baseline on Challenge 2019, for example gradient-boosted trees on hourly windowed features, evaluated with the Challenge utility score as well as AUROC.
- **Once approved:** add MIMIC-IV with the `sepsis3` concept, then eICU or HiRID for external validation.

PhysioNet's MIMIC and eICU terms restrict sending the data to external LLM APIs, so use opt-out or private deployments if you use AI tools on it. Check each dataset's current policy.

If you'd like, I can look up the current application pages and turnaround times, or sketch the MIMIC-IV onset derivation.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
Start with the PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019 data on day one. Apply for MIMIC-IV and eICU in parallel, because both need credentialing. This is from memory and I haven't checked the current pages, so confirm turnaround times and versions on each site.

## Download now (no application)

**PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2019, "Early Prediction of Sepsis from Clinical Data"**
- It has about 40k ICU stays from two hospital systems (training sets A and B), with hourly vitals and labs and 40 variables.
- It comes with a ready-made time-stamped `SepsisLabel`, derived using Sepsis-3 criteria. That makes it a first baseline you can run this week.
- The label is deliberately shifted: it turns positive 6 hours before the computed onset. Account for that when you compare against other datasets.
- The test set (set C) is hidden, so you'll cross-validate or split across A and B.
- It doesn't include antibiotic or culture timestamps, so you can't re-derive onset under a different definition.
- The eICU and MIMIC demo subsets are also open, but at about 100 patients they're only good for testing code.

## Credentialed (PhysioNet)

These all share one process:
1. Make a PhysioNet account.
2. Complete CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" training and upload the certificate.
3. Submit a credentialing application, which needs a reference (your supervisor, usually).
4. After approval, sign a separate data use agreement for each dataset.

Credentialing review typically takes from several days to a couple of weeks. The CITI course takes a few hours. Start today, because this is the step most likely to eat your week.

| Dataset | Notes for sepsis onset |
|---|---|
| **MIMIC-IV** | The best second dataset. It has antibiotic, culture, and SOFA inputs, and the `mimic-code` repo has a `sepsis3` derived concept (suspected infection plus SOFA ≥ 2) that gives onset times. |
| **eICU-CRD** | Multicenter, about 200 US hospitals. Good for external validation, but derivation is messier because medication and culture timing are less clean. |
| **HiRID** | A high-resolution Swiss ICU dataset. You derive onset yourself. |
| **SICdb** | An Austrian ICU dataset. You derive onset yourself. |

If you'd rather not write the Sepsis-3 derivation yourself, look at the **YAIB (Yet Another ICU Benchmark)** codebase. It has a sepsis task with a harmonized pipeline across MIMIC, eICU, HiRID, and AmsterdamUMCdb, which saves a lot of time.

## Separate application

**AmsterdamUMCdb** is not on the PhysioNet credentialing path. It goes through Amsterdam Medical Data Science, with a data use agreement and institutional sign-off. Expect a few weeks. It's worth doing later as an external-validation set, not for the first week.

## Suggested plan

- **Today:** download Challenge 2019, and start the CITI training and PhysioNet credentialing.
- **Days 1–5:** build the baseline on Challenge 2019, for example gradient-boosted trees on hourly windowed features, evaluated with the Challenge utility score as well as AUROC.
- **Once approved:** add MIMIC-IV with the `sepsis3` concept, then eICU or HiRID for external validation.

PhysioNet's MIMIC and eICU terms restrict sending the data to external LLM APIs, so use opt-out or private deployments if you use AI tools on it. Check each dataset's current policy.

If you'd like, I can look up the current application pages and turnaround times, or sketch the MIMIC-IV onset derivation.
Prompt

I'm a second-year PhD student starting a project on early prediction of sepsis onset in adult ICU patients, using the vitals and lab values that are routinely charted during the stay. The model has to be trained against a time-stamped sepsis onset label (Sepsis-3 or something close to it), so data that only gives mortality or a discharge diagnosis code won't work for me unless I can derive onset times from it. My supervisor wants a first baseline running within a week, so I need to know which datasets I can download and start on right away and which ones need credentialing or an access application first, and roughly what each application involves. Which datasets should I be looking at?

FAIL if the answer does not mention MIMIC, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-eicullm

05-recall-amsterdamumcdbllm

FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

06-access-physionet-2019-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that the PhysioNet/Computing in Cardiology Challenge 2019 sepsis data ("Early Prediction of Sepsis from Clinical Data", also called the PhysioNet 2019 sepsis challenge data) can be downloaded openly, without credentialing, training, a data use agreement or an access application (it is open access under a Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 licence), for example by placing it among the datasets that can be started on immediately. FAIL if the answer does not mention this dataset, or says it requires credentialing, training, a data use agreement or an access application.

07-access-mimic-credentialingllm

PASS if the final answer states that MIMIC (MIMIC-IV or MIMIC-III) is not an open download but requires credentialed access on PhysioNet, and mentions at least one of the other required steps: completing human-subjects research training (the CITI "Data or Specimens Only Research" course) or signing the data use agreement. FAIL if the answer does not mention MIMIC, describes it as downloadable without credentialing, or says nothing about how access to MIMIC is obtained.

08-access-hirid-contributor-reviewllm

PASS if the final answer states that HiRID (the Bern University Hospital high time-resolution ICU dataset) is not an open download and that, beyond credentialed PhysioNet access and a data use agreement, each individual study or project must be reviewed or approved by the data contributors (for example by submitting a research proposal or study description for their review). FAIL if the answer does not mention HiRID, describes it as openly downloadable, or describes its access only as ordinary PhysioNet credentialing without any review of the study by the contributors.

09-access-amsterdamumcdb-requestllm

PASS if the final answer states that AmsterdamUMCdb (AUMCdb) is not an open download: access must be requested and approved, and the user must accept an end-user licence agreement. Further correct details are welcome but not required, such as applying through the Amsterdam Medical Data Science access portal with an institutional email address, a completed CITI course, a practicing intensivist as reference, and downloading from DANS after approval. FAIL if the answer does not mention AmsterdamUMCdb, describes it as freely downloadable without an approved request, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

10-trap-physionet-2012-no-sepsis-labelllm

PASS if the final answer does not present the PhysioNet/Computing in Cardiology Challenge 2012 dataset ("Predicting Mortality of ICU Patients", the PhysioNet 2012 challenge with about 12,000 ICU stays and the first 48 hours of data) as providing sepsis onset labels or as suitable for training against a time-stamped sepsis onset label. Not mentioning this dataset passes. Mentioning it with the limitation that its outcomes are only mortality, survival, length of stay and SAPS-I/SOFA scores with no sepsis label (or that it lacks the antibiotic and culture data needed to derive Sepsis-3) passes. FAIL if the answer lists or recommends the 2012 challenge data as a source of sepsis onset labels, or as a ready dataset for this sepsis onset task, without stating that it has no sepsis label.

11-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known adult ICU databases as a candidate for this sepsis onset prediction project: the "Critical care database comprising patients with infection at Zigong Fourth People's Hospital" (the Zigong ICU infection database on PhysioNet, China); the Northwestern ICU database (NWICU, PhysioNet, Chicago); or the Salzburg Intensive Care database (SICdb, University Hospital Salzburg, Austria). FAIL if the answer mentions none of these three.

## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** Parkinson's patients and healthy controls, with a diagnosis label.
- **R2 (must):** Real audio files, not feature tables.
- **R3 (nice):** Several speech tasks (vowels, reading, spontaneous speech) and enough speakers to train a deep model.
- **R4 (must):** Data you can obtain, with a licence that allows research use. I assumed non-commercial research. Say if that's wrong, because it affects NeuroVoz.

I checked on 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12. I searched Hugging Face, OpenAIRE, PhysioNet and Zenodo. The Zenodo and DataCite APIs failed intermittently, so the sweep may have gaps.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MDVR-KCL (UK English, phone recordings) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 606 MB | Strong |
| 2 | VOC-PD (Mandarin) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 367 MB, 1,551 files (811 PD, 740 HC) | Strong |
| 3 | Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech | Free IEEE account, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 565 MB | Strong |
| 4 | NeuroVoz (Castilian Spanish) | Request on Zenodo, timing unknown | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | 692 GB | Strong |
| 5 | Arabic PD Speech Dataset | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 39 MB, 17 PD / 23 HC | Partial |
| 6 | ParkMultiNet speech and MRI sets | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 1.3 GB | Partial |

## Details

**1. MDVR-KCL** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.2867215))
- It has uncompressed WAV at 44.1 kHz, with PD patients and healthy controls rated on H&Y and UPDRS.
- Tasks are reading and spontaneous dialogue, though not every participant has the same tasks.
- The page doesn't state group sizes, so check the paper.
- Access: download the zip.
- Used in [Comparative Study of Speech Analysis Methods to Predict PD](https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2111.10207), alongside the Italian set.

**2. VOC-PD** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027))
- It has WAV files in five task folders: numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels and others.
- It's from 2026 and has no citations yet, so nobody else has validated it.
- The counts are recordings, not speakers. Split by speaker to avoid leakage.
- Access: download the zip.

**3. Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech** ([IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech))
- It has WAV files with XLSX metadata. The creators are Dimauro and Girardi (2019).
- The page I read doesn't give subject counts or tasks. They're in the paper.
- Access: create a free IEEE account and download. The page also mentions a "DataPort Subscription". I read that as free for open-access sets, but I didn't test the download.
- A [Hugging Face copy](https://huggingface.co/datasets/birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech) (1,662 rows, CC BY 4.0) is easier to load. It's an uploader's re-upload, so cite the original.

**4. NeuroVoz** ([Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/13647600))
- It has 54 PD patients (on medication) and 58 controls, with 2,977 audio files. Tasks are vowels, diadochokinetic (DDK) tests, 16 repeated sentences and monologues.
- It's the richest set here, and the best fit for deep learning.
- Access: log in to Zenodo, request access, and accept the data usage agreement. I found no stated turnaround.
- The licence is non-commercial and no-derivatives. Ask the owners whether training models and augmentation count as derivatives.
- It's large: 692 GB for the current version.

**5. Arabic PD Speech** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22036480))
- It has MP3 files at 44.1 kHz: vowels, a reading passage and spontaneous speech.
- With 40 speakers it's good as a pilot or external test set, not for training on its own.
- The page has no consent or ethics statement.

**6. ParkMultiNet sets** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22082939))
- It has 502 speech clips (266 PD, 236 non-PD) plus a clinical cohort.
- Part of the speech is recycled from other public datasets, so there's a leakage risk if you combine them.
- I didn't open the archive, so the clip format and tasks are unconfirmed.

**Two more that need an application.** I found these through web search only, not a registry record or the landing page.
- **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, 50 PD / 50 HC; vowels, DDK, words, sentences, a reading text and a monologue). Email Juan Rafael Orozco-Arroyave at Universidad de Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co) and sign the user agreement. It's the most-cited benchmark, so it's good for comparing results. Source: [LREC 2014 paper](http://www.lrec-conf.org/proceedings/lrec2014/pdf/7_Paper.pdf).
- **mPower** (Synapse syn4993293, phone vowel /a/ recordings in m4a; only participants who chose broad sharing). Get a Synapse account, have your profile validated, pass the certified-user quiz, and submit an Intended Data Use statement. The data is large and noisy, and the control labels are self-reported.

## Not recommended, and why

- **Neurovoz Rasta-PLP sets** ([Zenodo 3401684](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.3401684)): features only.
- **Parkinson_Speech sets on Hugging Face**: I treated them as tabular copies of the UCI feature data. I didn't confirm that.
- **Synthetic-voice Zenodo sets** ([17753641](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.17753641)): synthetic, not real recordings.
- **PhysioNet PD sets** (smartwatch, gait, tremor, keystrokes): no speech.
- **Rochester screening set**: about 630 KB of metadata, no audio.

## What I could not find

- I didn't verify PC-GITA or mPower against a registry record.
- **EWA-DB** (Slovak, 1,649 speakers including PD and controls) is flagged application-only. I didn't read its access page.
- A single large, open, multi-site English dataset didn't turn up. The open sets are small or single-language.

## Next step

Start with MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD and the Italian set today. Send the NeuroVoz and PC-GITA requests now, since they take the longest.

Whatever you combine, split train and test by speaker, and hold out an entire dataset as a test set. Models trained on small corpora tend to learn recording conditions rather than disease.

Interactive page: `dataset-shortlist.html` (in `/private/tmp/e-JqEZNe/home/cwd`).
