Email Juan Rafael Orozco-Arroyave at Universidad de Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co) and sign the user agreement. It's the most-cited benchmark, so it's good for comparing results. Source: [LREC 2014 paper](http://www.lrec-conf.org/proceedings/lrec2014/pdf/7_Paper.pdf).
- **mPower** (Synapse syn4993293, phone vowel /a/ recordings in m4a; only participants who chose broad sharing). Get a Synapse account, have your profile validated, pass the certified-user quiz, and submit an Intended Data Use statement. The data is large and noisy, and the control labels are self-reported.

## Not recommended, and why

- **Neurovoz Rasta-PLP sets** ([Zenodo 3401684](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.3401684)): features only.
- **Parkinson_Speech sets on Hugging Face**: I treated them as tabular copies of the UCI feature data. I didn't confirm that.
- **Synthetic-voice Zenodo sets** ([17753641](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.17753641)): synthetic, not real recordings.
- **PhysioNet PD sets** (smartwatch, gait, tremor, keystrokes): no speech.
- **Rochester screening set**: about 630 KB of metadata, no audio.

## What I could not find

- I didn't verify PC-GITA or mPower against a registry record.
- **EWA-DB** (Slovak, 1,649 speakers including PD and controls) is flagged application-only. I didn't read its access page.
- A single large, open, multi-site English dataset didn't turn up. The open sets are small or single-language.

## Next step

Start with MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD and the Italian set today. Send the NeuroVoz and PC-GITA requests now, since they take the longest.

Whatever you combine, split train and test by speaker, and hold out an entire dataset as a test set. Models trained on small corpora tend to learn recording conditions rather than disease.

Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** Parkinson's patients and healthy controls, with a diagnosis label.
- **R2 (must):** Real audio files, not feature tables.
- **R3 (nice):** Several speech tasks (vowels, reading, spontaneous speech) and enough speakers to train a deep model.
- **R4 (must):** Data you can obtain, with a licence that allows research use. I assumed non-commercial research. Say if that's wrong, because it affects NeuroVoz.

I checked on 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12. I searched Hugging Face, OpenAIRE, PhysioNet and Zenodo. The Zenodo and DataCite APIs failed intermittently, so the sweep may have gaps.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MDVR-KCL (UK English, phone recordings) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 606 MB | Strong |
| 2 | VOC-PD (Mandarin) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 367 MB, 1,551 files (811 PD, 740 HC) | Strong |
| 3 | Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech | Free IEEE account, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 565 MB | Strong |
| 4 | NeuroVoz (Castilian Spanish) | Request on Zenodo, timing unknown | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | 692 GB | Strong |
| 5 | Arabic PD Speech Dataset | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 39 MB, 17 PD / 23 HC | Partial |
| 6 | ParkMultiNet speech and MRI sets | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 1.3 GB | Partial |

## Details

**1. MDVR-KCL** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.2867215))
- It has uncompressed WAV at 44.1 kHz, with PD patients and healthy controls rated on H&Y and UPDRS.
- Tasks are reading and spontaneous dialogue, though not every participant has the same tasks.
- The page doesn't state group sizes, so check the paper.
- Access: download the zip.
- Used in [Comparative Study of Speech Analysis Methods to Predict PD](https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2111.10207), alongside the Italian set.

**2. VOC-PD** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027))
- It has WAV files in five task folders: numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels and others.
- It's from 2026 and has no citations yet, so nobody else has validated it.
- The counts are recordings, not speakers. Split by speaker to avoid leakage.
- Access: download the zip.

**3. Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech** ([IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech))
- It has WAV files with XLSX metadata. The creators are Dimauro and Girardi (2019).
- The page I read doesn't give subject counts or tasks. They're in the paper.
- Access: create a free IEEE account and download. The page also mentions a "DataPort Subscription". I read that as free for open-access sets, but I didn't test the download.
- A [Hugging Face copy](https://huggingface.co/datasets/birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech) (1,662 rows, CC BY 4.0) is easier to load. It's an uploader's re-upload, so cite the original.

**4. NeuroVoz** ([Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/13647600))
- It has 54 PD patients (on medication) and 58 controls, with 2,977 audio files. Tasks are vowels, diadochokinetic (DDK) tests, 16 repeated sentences and monologues.
- It's the richest set here, and the best fit for deep learning.
- Access: log in to Zenodo, request access, and accept the data usage agreement. I found no stated turnaround.
- The licence is non-commercial and no-derivatives. Ask the owners whether training models and augmentation count as derivatives.
- It's large: 692 GB for the current version.

**5. Arabic PD Speech** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22036480))
- It has MP3 files at 44.1 kHz: vowels, a reading passage and spontaneous speech.
- With 40 speakers it's good as a pilot or external test set, not for training on its own.
- The page has no consent or ethics statement.

**6. ParkMultiNet sets** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22082939))
- It has 502 speech clips (266 PD, 236 non-PD) plus a clinical cohort.
- Part of the speech is recycled from other public datasets, so there's a leakage risk if you combine them.
- I didn't open the archive, so the clip format and tasks are unconfirmed.

**Two more that need an application.** I found these through web search only, not a registry record or the landing page.
- **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, 50 PD / 50 HC; vowels, DDK, words, sentences, a reading text and a monologue). Email Juan Rafael Orozco-Arroyave at Universidad de Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co) and sign the user agreement. It's the most-cited benchmark, so it's good for comparing results. Source: [LREC 2014 paper](http://www.lrec-conf.org/proceedings/lrec2014/pdf/7_Paper.pdf).
- **mPower** (Synapse syn4993293, phone vowel /a/ recordings in m4a; only participants who chose broad sharing). Get a Synapse account, have your profile validated, pass the certified-user quiz, and submit an Intended Data Use statement. The data is large and noisy, and the control labels are self-reported.

## Not recommended, and why

- **Neurovoz Rasta-PLP sets** ([Zenodo 3401684](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.3401684)): features only.
- **Parkinson_Speech sets on Hugging Face**: I treated them as tabular copies of the UCI feature data. I didn't confirm that.
- **Synthetic-voice Zenodo sets** ([17753641](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.17753641)): synthetic, not real recordings.
- **PhysioNet PD sets** (smartwatch, gait, tremor, keystrokes): no speech.
- **Rochester screening set**: about 630 KB of metadata, no audio.

## What I could not find

- I didn't verify PC-GITA or mPower against a registry record.
- **EWA-DB** (Slovak, 1,649 speakers including PD and controls) is flagged application-only. I didn't read its access page.
- A single large, open, multi-site English dataset didn't turn up. The open sets are small or single-language.

- **EWA-DB** (Slovak, 1,649 speakers including PD and controls) is flagged application-only. I didn't read its access page.
- A single large, open, multi-site English dataset didn't turn up. The open sets are small or single-language.

## Next step

Start with MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD and the Italian set today. Send the NeuroVoz and PC-GITA requests now, since they take the longest.

Whatever you combine, split train and test by speaker, and hold out an entire dataset as a test set. Models trained on small corpora tend to learn recording conditions rather than disease.

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
## What I assumed you need

- **R1 (must):** Parkinson's patients and healthy controls, with a diagnosis label.
- **R2 (must):** Real audio files, not feature tables.
- **R3 (nice):** Several speech tasks (vowels, reading, spontaneous speech) and enough speakers to train a deep model.
- **R4 (must):** Data you can obtain, with a licence that allows research use. I assumed non-commercial research. Say if that's wrong, because it affects NeuroVoz.

I checked on 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12. I searched Hugging Face, OpenAIRE, PhysioNet and Zenodo. The Zenodo and DataCite APIs failed intermittently, so the sweep may have gaps.

## Shortlist

| # | Dataset | Access | Licence | Size | Fit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | MDVR-KCL (UK English, phone recordings) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 606 MB | Strong |
| 2 | VOC-PD (Mandarin) | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 367 MB, 1,551 files (811 PD, 740 HC) | Strong |
| 3 | Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech | Free IEEE account, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 565 MB | Strong |
| 4 | NeuroVoz (Castilian Spanish) | Request on Zenodo, timing unknown | CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 | 692 GB | Strong |
| 5 | Arabic PD Speech Dataset | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 39 MB, 17 PD / 23 HC | Partial |
| 6 | ParkMultiNet speech and MRI sets | Direct download, minutes | CC BY 4.0 | 1.3 GB | Partial |

## Details

**1. MDVR-KCL** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.2867215))
- It has uncompressed WAV at 44.1 kHz, with PD patients and healthy controls rated on H&Y and UPDRS.
- Tasks are reading and spontaneous dialogue, though not every participant has the same tasks.
- The page doesn't state group sizes, so check the paper.
- Access: download the zip.
- Used in [Comparative Study of Speech Analysis Methods to Predict PD](https://doi.org/10.48550/arxiv.2111.10207), alongside the Italian set.

**2. VOC-PD** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21775027))
- It has WAV files in five task folders: numbers, phrases, sentences, vowels and others.
- It's from 2026 and has no citations yet, so nobody else has validated it.
- The counts are recordings, not speakers. Split by speaker to avoid leakage.
- Access: download the zip.

**3. Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech** ([IEEE DataPort](https://ieee-dataport.org/open-access/italian-parkinsons-voice-and-speech))
- It has WAV files with XLSX metadata. The creators are Dimauro and Girardi (2019).
- The page I read doesn't give subject counts or tasks. They're in the paper.
- Access: create a free IEEE account and download. The page also mentions a "DataPort Subscription". I read that as free for open-access sets, but I didn't test the download.
- A [Hugging Face copy](https://huggingface.co/datasets/birgermoell/Italian_Parkinsons_Voice_and_Speech) (1,662 rows, CC BY 4.0) is easier to load. It's an uploader's re-upload, so cite the original.

**4. NeuroVoz** ([Zenodo](https://zenodo.org/records/13647600))
- It has 54 PD patients (on medication) and 58 controls, with 2,977 audio files. Tasks are vowels, diadochokinetic (DDK) tests, 16 repeated sentences and monologues.
- It's the richest set here, and the best fit for deep learning.
- Access: log in to Zenodo, request access, and accept the data usage agreement. I found no stated turnaround.
- The licence is non-commercial and no-derivatives. Ask the owners whether training models and augmentation count as derivatives.
- It's large: 692 GB for the current version.

**5. Arabic PD Speech** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22036480))
- It has MP3 files at 44.1 kHz: vowels, a reading passage and spontaneous speech.
- With 40 speakers it's good as a pilot or external test set, not for training on its own.
- The page has no consent or ethics statement.

**6. ParkMultiNet sets** ([Zenodo](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.22082939))
- It has 502 speech clips (266 PD, 236 non-PD) plus a clinical cohort.
- Part of the speech is recycled from other public datasets, so there's a leakage risk if you combine them.
- I didn't open the archive, so the clip format and tasks are unconfirmed.

**Two more that need an application.** I found these through web search only, not a registry record or the landing page.
- **PC-GITA** (Colombian Spanish, 50 PD / 50 HC; vowels, DDK, words, sentences, a reading text and a monologue). Email Juan Rafael Orozco-Arroyave at Universidad de Antioquia (rafael.orozco@udea.edu.co) and sign the user agreement. It's the most-cited benchmark, so it's good for comparing results. Source: [LREC 2014 paper](http://www.lrec-conf.org/proceedings/lrec2014/pdf/7_Paper.pdf).
- **mPower** (Synapse syn4993293, phone vowel /a/ recordings in m4a; only participants who chose broad sharing). Get a Synapse account, have your profile validated, pass the certified-user quiz, and submit an Intended Data Use statement. The data is large and noisy, and the control labels are self-reported.

## Not recommended, and why

- **Neurovoz Rasta-PLP sets** ([Zenodo 3401684](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.3401684)): features only.
- **Parkinson_Speech sets on Hugging Face**: I treated them as tabular copies of the UCI feature data. I didn't confirm that.
- **Synthetic-voice Zenodo sets** ([17753641](https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.17753641)): synthetic, not real recordings.
- **PhysioNet PD sets** (smartwatch, gait, tremor, keystrokes): no speech.
- **Rochester screening set**: about 630 KB of metadata, no audio.

## What I could not find

- I didn't verify PC-GITA or mPower against a registry record.
- **EWA-DB** (Slovak, 1,649 speakers including PD and controls) is flagged application-only. I didn't read its access page.
- A single large, open, multi-site English dataset didn't turn up. The open sets are small or single-language.

## Next step

Start with MDVR-KCL, VOC-PD and the Italian set today. Send the NeuroVoz and PC-GITA requests now, since they take the longest.

Whatever you combine, split train and test by speaker, and hold out an entire dataset as a test set. Models trained on small corpora tend to learn recording conditions rather than disease.

Interactive page: `dataset-shortlist.html` (in `/private/tmp/e-JqEZNe/home/cwd`).

✓ pass 01-recall-italian-parkinsons-voice-speech

judge votes: PASS PASS PASS

Judge votes✓ ✓ ✓
Evidence (what the judge was shown; for an image, a description of what was sent)
This list is from memory and I haven't checked the links or access terms today. Confirm each one before you build on it. Only datasets that ship real audio are included. The UCI/Oxford and Sakar sets are features only, so skip them.

| Dataset | Language / size | Access |
|---|---|---|
| **Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech (IPVS)** | Italian, about 65 subjects (28 PD, 37 HC), several hundred recordings of vowels, phrases and sentences | Free on IEEE DataPort, which needs a free account. Some copies are mirrored on Kaggle, but check that the license and provenance match. |
| **MDVR-KCL** (King's College London / King Abdullah University Hospital) | English/Arabic-region cohort, about 37 subjects (16 PD, 21 HC), smartphone recordings of reading and spontaneous speech | Open on Zenodo. Search "Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London". |
| **mPower** (Sage Bionetworks) | Thousands of smartphone "aaah" recordings, with PD and control labels self-reported through the app | Register on Synapse, complete the qualified-researcher process, and accept the data use terms. Labels are noisy, and you'll need to convert the m4a files. |
| **PC-GITA** | Colombian Spanish, 50 PD and 50 HC, vowels, words, sentences and monologue | Not openly downloadable. Email the GITA group at Universidad de Antioquia to request it under a license agreement. It's the most widely benchmarked set, so it's worth the effort. |
| **NeuroVoz** | Castilian Spanish, about 100+ PD and about 50+ HC, with several speech tasks | Distributed on Zenodo, as I recall under a request or restricted-access arrangement. Check the paper (LREC-COLING 2024) for the current process. |
| **Bridge2AI-Voice** | Multi-condition, including Parkinson's | On PhysioNet, which needs credentialed access and a signed DUA. The public releases have been derived features and spectrograms rather than raw audio, so check whether the current version includes waveforms. |

**Practical notes**
- **Size:** All of these are small, apart from mPower. Use subject-wise splits, not recording-wise, or you'll leak speakers across train and test.
- **Confounds:** Recording device, channel and session effects can separate PD from HC in these sets. Models can pick up the microphone instead of the disease. Cross-corpus evaluation, for example training on one set and testing on another, is the honest check.
- **Combining sets:** Mixing languages and tasks is common (domain adaptation work does it), but label consistently by task type.
- **Not PD:** TORGO and UA-Speech are dysarthria corpora (cerebral palsy/ALS), not Parkinson's. They're only useful for pretraining.

I can search to verify the current links and access terms for any of these, or look for more recent releases like Mendeley/Figshare uploads. Do you want me to?

| **Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech (IPVS)** | Italian, about 65 subjects (28 PD, 37 HC), several hundred recordings of vowels, phrases and sentences | Free on IEEE DataPort, which needs a free account. Some copies are mirrored on Kaggle, but check that the license and provenance match. |
| **MDVR-KCL** (King's College London / King Abdullah University Hospital) | English/Arabic-region cohort, about 37 subjects (16 PD, 21 HC), smartphone recordings of reading and spontaneous speech | Open on Zenodo. Search "Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London". |
| **mPower** (Sage Bionetworks) | Thousands of smartphone "aaah" recordings, with PD and control labels self-reported through the app | Register on Synapse, complete the qualified-researcher process, and accept the data use terms. Labels are noisy, and you'll need to convert the m4a files. |
| **PC-GITA** | Colombian Spanish, 50 PD and 50 HC, vowels, words, sentences and monologue | Not openly downloadable. Email the GITA group at Universidad de Antioquia to request it under a license agreement. It's the most widely benchmarked set, so it's worth the effort. |
| **NeuroVoz** | Castilian Spanish, about 100+ PD and about 50+ HC, with several speech tasks | Distributed on Zenodo, as I recall under a request or restricted-access arrangement. Check the paper (LREC-COLING 2024) for the current process. |
| **Bridge2AI-Voice** | Multi-condition, including Parkinson's | On PhysioNet, which needs credentialed access and a signed DUA. The public releases have been derived features and spectrograms rather than raw audio, so check whether the current version includes waveforms. |

**Practical notes**
- **Size:** All of these are small, apart from mPower. Use subject-wise splits, not recording-wise, or you'll leak speakers across train and test.
- **Confounds:** Recording device, channel and session effects can separate PD from HC in these sets. Models can pick up the microphone instead of the disease. Cross-corpus evaluation, for example training on one set and testing on another, is the honest check.
- **Combining sets:** Mixing languages and tasks is common (domain adaptation work does it), but label consistently by task type.
- **Not PD:** TORGO and UA-Speech are dysarthria corpora (cerebral palsy/ALS), not Parkinson's. They're only useful for pretraining.

I can search to verify the current links and access terms for any of these, or look for more recent releases like Mendeley/Figshare uploads. Do you want me to?

Prompt

hey, I'm looking for public datasets of voice or speech recordings from people with Parkinson's disease plus healthy controls, for a deep learning detection project. I need the actual audio files, not tables of precomputed acoustic features. What's out there and how do I get access to each one?

Graders — what "good" means for this case
00-skill-firedtool_used
tool"Skill"
input_match"\"skill\"\\s*:\\s*\"(?:[\\w-]+:)?dataset-scout\""
01-recall-italian-parkinsons-voice-speechllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the "Italian Parkinson's Voice and Speech" dataset (also known as IPVS or the Italian PD voice dataset by Dimauro and Girardi, University of Bari, hosted on IEEE DataPort) as a candidate source of raw speech audio from people with Parkinson's disease and healthy controls. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

02-recall-mdvr-kclllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the "Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London" dataset (also known as MDVR-KCL, smartphone recordings of people with early and advanced Parkinson's disease and healthy controls, hosted on Zenodo) as a candidate source of raw speech audio for Parkinson's detection. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

03-recall-mpowerllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the mPower dataset (also known as the Parkinson mPower mobile study from Sage Bionetworks, distributed through Synapse, whose voice activity contains smartphone recordings of a sustained "aaah") as a candidate source of voice recordings from people with Parkinson's disease and controls. FAIL if the answer does not mention mPower, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

04-recall-bridge2ai-voicellm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists the Bridge2AI-Voice dataset (also known as B2AI-Voice, the Bridge2AI Voice adult dataset, published on PhysioNet as b2ai-voice, which includes participants with neurological disorders such as Parkinson's disease) as a candidate dataset for voice-based Parkinson's detection. FAIL if the answer does not mention it, or mentions it only to say it is unsuitable.

05-access-mdvr-kcl-openllm

PASS if the final answer states that MDVR-KCL (Mobile Device Voice Recordings at King's College London) can be downloaded openly from Zenodo without an application or approval. If the answer names its licence, the licence must be Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 (CC BY 4.0). FAIL if the answer does not mention MDVR-KCL, says it must be requested or approved before download, or gives a different licence (for example a non-commercial one).

06-access-mpower-qualified-researcherllm

PASS if the final answer states that mPower (the Parkinson mPower study data on Synapse) is not an open download but is released only to approved, qualified researchers through Synapse, and mentions at least one of the required steps: registering a Synapse account, passing the Synapse certification quiz, validating the user profile (for example with ORCID and an identity attestation letter), submitting an Intended Data Use statement, or agreeing to the conditions for use (which forbid redistribution). FAIL if the answer does not mention mPower, describes it as freely downloadable, or says nothing about how access to it is obtained.

07-access-bridge2ai-no-raw-audiollm

PASS if the final answer states that the Bridge2AI-Voice dataset released on PhysioNet does not contain the raw audio recordings, only derived data such as spectrograms, MFCCs or other acoustic features. Adding that raw audio requires a separate, more involved request (institutional sign-off through the project's data access committee, with data delivered via Synapse) is welcome but not required. FAIL if the answer does not mention Bridge2AI-Voice, presents its PhysioNet release as providing the raw audio files, or recommends it as a source of audio without saying that the raw recordings are not in the standard release.

08-trap-uci-parkinsons-features-onlyllm

PASS if the final answer does not present the UCI Machine Learning Repository "Parkinsons" dataset (also called the Oxford Parkinson's Disease Detection Dataset by Max Little, 2007: 195 sustained-phonation recordings from 31 people, given as a CSV of 22 voice measures such as jitter, shimmer and HNR) as a source of raw audio recordings. Not mentioning it passes. Mentioning it while saying that it contains only precomputed acoustic features and no audio files passes. FAIL if the answer recommends or lists this UCI dataset as providing the audio recordings the user asked for, or lists it as a candidate without saying that it has no audio.

09-long-tailllm

PASS if the final answer recommends or lists at least one of these less widely known Parkinson's speech datasets with audio recordings: NeuroVoz (the Castilian Spanish corpus of parkinsonian speech on Zenodo); EWA-DB (the Slovak "Early Warning of Alzheimer" speech database, which includes people with Parkinson's disease); or the figshare dataset "Voice Samples for Patients with Parkinson's Disease and Healthy Controls" (sustained /a/ recordings made on participants' telephones, by Iyer, Prior, Virmani and colleagues). FAIL if the answer mentions none of these three.
